Lens 1, QA

Corrupted save with an unknown hull crashes on load/map.
core.js:70 — Array.isArray(r.fleet)&&!!r.map&&Array.isArray(r.map.cols) — OBSERVED. Save validation checks only that fleet is an array, not each ship’s tpl. core.js:180 then does RUN.fleet.reduce((a,s)=>a+HULLS[s.tpl].cp,0) with no guard. A renamed/removed hull in a save throws before the player can act.

Decommissioning the flagship leaves RUN.flagUid stale.
campaign_run.js:181 — RUN.fleet.splice(i,1); closeModal(); shipyardScreen(yardNode); — OBSERVED. There is no ensureFlagship() or flagUid update. battle.js:113 uses opts.ship.uid===RUN.flagUid for pflag, so the next battle can have no flagship and no aspect applied.

Captured boss can be added to the player fleet.
battle.js:676 — t.side=u.side; t.crew=Math.max(3,Math.round(t.crewMax*.4)); t.captured=true; — INFERRED. boardUnit has no boss exclusion; any non-squadron can be captured if crew reaches 0. campaign3d.js:81 then does const sh=newShip(u.tpl); and pushes it into RUN.fleet. Boss hulls like h_boss have no cost/slots set by the roster loop, so this can create NaN sell values and blow past command points.

Unknown relic id in a save crashes the map screen.
campaign_run.js:46 — esc(RELICS[r].d) — OBSERVED. normalizeRun does not validate relic ids. unknownId exists for derive-time relic effects, but map rendering dereferences RELICS[r] directly, so a renamed/removed relic id throws when Continue opens the map.

Saved captain with a missing name crashes ship creation.
core.js:126 — s.cap&&s.cap.name.split(' ')[0] — OBSERVED. normalizeRun only creates s.cap if it is absent/not an object; it does not fill cap.name. A save with cap:{} passes saneSave, then capName throws when buying a hull, hiring an event ship, or creating a replacement.

A zero-fleet win is converted into a run loss.
battle.js:771 — if(caps('enemy').length===0){ B.over='win'; return; } — OBSERVED. Enemy annihilation is checked before player annihilation. If ticks leave both sides at zero, B.over is win; then campaign3d.js:88 — if(result==='loss' || RUN.fleet.length===0){ endRun(false); return; } — records defeat anyway.

Towing salvage ignores the command-point cap.
campaign_run.js:272 — RUN.fleet.push(sh); — OBSERVED. The tow path creates and adds a ship with no fleetCP()+HULLS[tpl].cp<=RUN.cpCap check, unlike ACT.buyHull at campaign_run.js:174. A Corsair can exceed cpCap through salvage.

Nova cooldown is consumed even when the blast is abandoned after a stall.
battle.js:953 — const c=arg.hex; u.abCd=abilityCd(u); — OBSERVED. The cooldown is set before battle.js:955 — if(!live()) return false;. If the animation stalls, the nova never lands but the ability is spent.

LEGACY upgrades have no UI route.
campaign_meta.js:113 — ['heat','Heat'],['fallen','Hall of the fallen'] — OBSERVED. LEGACY defines war chests, command authority, veteran officers, founder relic, shipwrights and salvagers, and newRun checks META.unlocked.chest1, cmd1, etc. But legacyScreen renders only variants, mastery, favour, heat and fallen; ACT.buyLegacy has no button.

Reserve captains override event mercenary veterans.
campaign3d.js:99 — const r=RUN.reserve.shift(); sh.cap=r.cap; — OBSERVED. The patched newShip replaces the captain of any new ship when no battle is active. campaign_run.js:215 — const s=newShip(tp,r.vet); — expects the mercenary event to create a veteran captain, but a stored reserve captain silently wins.

Key architectural observations:

Everything is global scope, concatenated by build.py. build.py:8 concatenates a list of files into one <script> tag. All top-level const/let/function are globals. build.py:11 only checks duplicate functions, not duplicate const/let/var/class. Actually wait — build.py:11 regex is ^(?:async\s+)?function\s+... — only functions. smoke.py:15 does check const|let|var|class too. But build.py itself only checks functions. So duplicate const declarations across files would be a SyntaxError at parse time (let/const redeclaration is a SyntaxError), which node --check catches. Actually, duplicate const in same scope is a SyntaxError, so node --check would catch it. But duplicate var or duplicate let? let duplicate is SyntaxError. var duplicate is allowed. So build.py's function-only check is mostly redundant with node --check for const/let, but not for var. Still, the deeper issue: no modules, no namespacing.

Monkey-patching via reassignment of globals. campaign3d.js:10 — show=function(html){...} reassigns the global show defined in campaign_meta.js:6. campaign3d.js:31 — mapScreen=function(){...} reassigns mapScreen from campaign_run.js:25. campaign3d.js:34 — titleScreen=function(){...}. campaign3d.js:50,99 — _fb=finishBattle style in smoke. campaign3d.js:99 — const _newShip=newShip; newShip=function(t,vet){...}. This is a fragile extension pattern: load order determines behavior, and any file that reassigns must be loaded after. build.py:8 order matters silently.

show reassignment ordering bug. campaign_meta.js:6 defines function show(html). campaign3d.js:10 — show=function(html){ ... _show(html); ...} where _show=show captured the original. But wait: const _show=show; show=function(html){...} — _show captures the function value at that moment. Since function declarations are hoisted, show is the function. OK. But campaign_meta.js also has modal etc. Fine. However campaign3d.js loads after campaign_meta.js in build order (line 8: campaign_meta.js before ui3d.js before campaign3d.js). So reassignment works. But it's implicit and fragile.

startSideTurn returns a promise but many callers ignore it. battle.js:353 — function startSideTurn(side){ returns deaths.length?settleDeaths(deaths):null;. battle.js:379 — return deaths.length?settleDeaths(deaths):null;. Many call sites don't await: battle.js:339 — startSideTurn('player'); (no await) in startBattle. battle.js:358 gainResolve. sim3d.js:39 calls startSideTurn without await. battle.js:544 — const dq2=startSideTurn('player'); if(dq2) await dq2; — good. That's a real ordering hazard.

startBattle calls startSideTurn('player') without awaiting, at the end. battle.js:339 — startSideTurn('player');. If a player ship dies from a tick at battle start (e.g., acid/fire from a previous... no, fresh units), unlikely. But it's a pattern: fire-and-forget async.

aiTurn / aiUnit await chain but B.over checks scattered. Hard to prove.

FLEET_LISTS duplicated across ui3d.js and sim3d.js. ui3d.js:25-32 and sim3d.js:48-55 — identical hand-built fleets. The sim is supposed to test the game, but it carries a copy that can drift. This is a big one for "what breaks first": the sim will validate a stale fleet composition.

The biggest engineering finding: there is no module system, no imports, no exports, and the entire game is one concatenated script with ~18 files and thousands of globals. This is the core architectural debt. Every "vXX" comment in the source is a patch. The build.py duplicate-function check is the only static guard, and it misses everything that isn't a plain function declaration.

Patching style: version-tagged monkey patches. campaign3d.js:10,31,34,50,99; ui3d.js:2,3; battle.js v53 comments. core.js:1-3 comment says "v41: seeded rules randomness". This is the "patching style" the lens asks about. Each feature is bolted on by reassigning a global. What breaks first: load order. If campaign3d.js is moved before ui3d.js in build.py:8, show reassignment still works but mapScreen wrapper references setupFleetShowcase (defined in campaign3d) — fine. But ui3d.js top-level references ACT (from campaign_meta) and SETUP (own). campaign_run.js references nodeSeeds (campaign3d) inside mapScreen at line 34 — if(nodeSeeds(...)). nodeSeeds is defined in campaign3d.js:46. campaign_run.js loads before campaign3d.js. Since mapScreen isn't called at load, and nodeSeeds is a hoisted function declaration in campaign3d.js (loaded later but in same concatenated script, so hoisted to top of the single script scope), it's available. Actually all function declarations across all concatenated files are hoisted into the single global scope at parse time. So nodeSeeds is callable from campaign_run's mapScreen. But const _map=mapScreen at campaign3d:30 captures... hmm.

Key: because everything is one script, function declarations hoist globally. That's why the patching "works". But const/let are in temporal dead zone until their line executes. const COMMANDS=... in battle.js executes at its line. Fine.

build.py writes index_test.html or voidsworn-3d.html; no source maps, no minification, no cache-busting. build.py:17-19. Shipped file contains all comments and the full Babylon CDN. For a browser game on phones, the single HTML is huge. But more importantly, the shipped build uses a different Babylon source than tested. build.py:3.

smoke.py check('no duplicate globals',...) uses a different regex than build.py and scans FILES (14 files) which excludes meshopt3d.js that build.py includes. build.py:8 includes meshopt3d.js first; smoke.py:6 FILES starts at data.js. So a duplicate between meshopt3d.js and another file is not caught by smoke's scan (though node --check catches const/let). Also build.py checks only functions. Discrepancy.

sim3d.js is 575 lines of test harness evaled alongside the game, with its own copy of FLEET_LISTS and its own botTurn/botUnit/match. It's effectively a second implementation of a player AI. sim3d.js:63-78 botUnit. sim3d.js:16-28 botTurn. This will drift from the real aiUnit. When the AI changes (v53-D), the bot is not updated, so balance sims measure an outdated player.

battle.js is 1143 lines, data.js 448, ui3d.js 724, campaign_run.js 321. God-files. battle.js mixes rules, AI, objectives, abilities, commands, squadron logic, terrain generation. Splitting is the restructure.

derive(ship) recomputes fleet-wide fx every call. core.js:136 fleetFxList() iterates all relics, admiral fx, all boons and calls BOONS[b.id].fx(RARITY[...].m) each time. derive is called in fleetPower for every ship every time, in newShip, in changeShip, in UI rendering per ship per frame? refreshPanel calls derive? It uses u.maxHp from unit, not derive. But fleetScreen calls derive(s) per ship. mkCost calls derive. Performance, but the lens is architecture.

fx accumulator sumFx uses object merge, so hangar and hive are summed, but maxHp uses 1+(fx.hpPct||0). Fine.

**nameWeapons mutates the weapon template via WTRAITS[tk].f(w) on a fresh Object.assign({id,arc},b) — so b (the W entry) is not mutated because mkWeapon copies. Good. But nameWeapons(t.fac, ...) in makeUnit line 103 uses t.fac which may be undefined for shared squadrons (fighter has no fac). Then FAC_WTRAIT[undefined] → undefined, no trait. Fine.

makeUnit line 105 fac: t.fac || opts.fac || 'voidsworn'. For a shared fighter squadron with no fac, it becomes 'voidsworn'. Then CREW_MULT doesn't apply (isSq). OK.

Duplication of armor/stat logic between makeUnit and derive. battle.js:100-103 builds s from t with opts.scale, ignoring modules/perks; core.js:163 derive for player ships. Two stat pipelines. A module added to a player ship is reflected in derive but enemy scaling uses a separate path. When a new stat is added it must be added in both.

B.usage telemetry object keys are ad-hoc strings; B.usage.cant categories via regex on English text. ui3d.js:412 — /braced/.test(why)?'braced':.... Localization or wording change breaks analytics. OBSERVED.

stallGuard uses setInterval and Promise.race; the interval is cleared in .finally, but the lim promise never rejects; if the guarded promise throws, .catch logs and resolves undefined. battle.js:813-818. Promise.race([p.catch(...),lim]). If p throws, the race resolves with undefined, and callers treat that as success. swarmPhase line 900 checks ==='stall' only. So an action error silently continues. That's an engineering risk.

smoke.py builds then reads index_test.html and re-extracts JS via string slicing s[s.index('<script>\n')+9:s.rindex('</script>')]. smoke.py:10. Fragile to HTML changes.

Lens 2, ENGINEER

The whole game is one concatenated global script; there are no modules, imports, or exports. build.py:8 — ''.join(open(f).read()+'\n' for f in ['meshopt3d.js','data.js','core.js','battle.js', — OBSERVED. Eighteen files are pasted into one <script> at build.py:17. Every top-level const, let, and function is a global; name collisions are resolved by load order or become a parse error. Restructure this first: ES modules (or IIFE namespaces) plus a real bundler. Until then every new feature is a global-scope negotiation.

Features are added by reassigning globals; load order is the real dependency graph. campaign3d.js:10 — show=function(html){ showApp(); appEl.classList.remove('see3d'); _show(html); and campaign3d.js:99 — const _newShip=newShip; newShip=function(t,vet){ const sh=_newShip(t,vet); — OBSERVED. mapScreen, titleScreen, show, and newShip are all wrapped by reassignment, each capturing the previous value. This only works because build.py:8 loads campaign_meta.js/campaign_run.js/ui3d.js before campaign3d.js. A reorder, a second wrapper, or a call during load silently breaks. Replace with explicit hooks/events or an extension point registered by name.

One mutable B/RUN plus fire-and-forget async, defended by ad-hoc epoch and stall guards. battle.js:339 — startSideTurn('player'); (not awaited at battle start), ui3d.js:50 — if(B.alt) startSideTurn('enemy'); (not awaited in prep), and ui3d.js:579 — const d1=startSideTurn('player'), d2=B.over?null:startSideTurn('enemy'); if(d1) await d1; — OBSERVED. startSideTurn returns a promise for tick deaths (battle.js:379), but several call sites drop it, and nextRound starts both sides before awaiting either. liveFrom/inThisBattle/B.epoch/stallGuard exist only because a single global B cannot isolate a battle. Make battle state an object passed through the turn engine; make every startSideTurn awaited.

Two independent stat pipelines: makeUnit for enemies, derive for player ships. battle.js:101 — s={maxHp:Math.round(t.hp*sc*(opts.elite?1.1:1)), shMax:Math.round(t.sh*sc), regen:Math.round(t.rg*sc), armor:t.ar, versus core.js:168 — const maxHp=Math.round(t.hp*mm*(1+(fx.hpPct||0))); — OBSERVED. Adding a stat (armor pierce, regen, a new module fx) requires editing both paths or enemies and players diverge. This is the most likely silent balance bug as features accrue.

The simulator carries its own copy of the hand-built fleets. sim3d.js:49 — voidsworn:{sk:['cathedral','penitent','reliquary','harrow','warden','warden'],fl:[ duplicates ui3d.js:26 verbatim — OBSERVED. Change a skirmish fleet in ui3d.js and every balance sim still measures the old composition. FLEET_LISTS belongs in data.js (or a shared module) imported by both.

COMMANDS in battle.js is a dead duplicate of the admiral cmd objects. battle.js:822 — const COMMANDS={vex:{id:'barrage',n:'Fire for effect',d:'Every ship fires a free volley — OBSERVED. data.js:209-212 assigns .cmd to vex, malek, ashgrove and thale, and battle.js:844 prefers a.cmd||COMMANDS[id]. So COMMANDS is unreachable for every entry it defines. Editing it looks like a balance change but does nothing.

The build-time static check and the smoke-time static check disagree, and both are weaker than they look. build.py:11 — re.finditer(r'^(?:async\s+)?function\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*\(',js,re.M) catches only plain function declarations, while smoke.py:6 — FILES=['data.js','core.js','battle.js','scene3d.js','ships3d.js','fx3d.js','sfx_data.js', omits meshopt3d.js, which build.py:8 includes. OBSERVED. smoke.py:15 scans const|let|var|class but over a different file set. Duplicate arrow-function bindings or a collision in meshopt3d.js pass both checks until runtime.

What is tested is not what ships: the build selects a different Babylon source by mode. build.py:3 — src='node_modules/babylonjs/babylon.js' if mode=='test' else 'https://cdnjs... — OBSERVED. smoke.py and sim3d.js exercise the local node_modules Babylon; voidsworn-3d.html loads the CDN copy. A CDN version bump or a stale local copy is invisible to the test suite.

sim3d.js reimplements the presentation layer and a player AI, so it will drift from the game it is meant to test. sim3d.js:3 — eval(['data.js','core.js','battle.js'].map(f=>fs.readFileSync(f,'utf8')).join('\n')+ followed by hand-written stubs (sim3d.js:5 — async function animMove(u,q,r,f){ u.q=q; u.r=r; u.facing=f;) and sim3d.js:63 — async function botUnit(u){ — OBSERVED. stallGuard converts an action exception into a normal resolution. battle.js:818 — return Promise.race([Promise.resolve(p).catch(e=>{ try{ if(typeof tel==='function') tel('action_error', — OBSERVED. If the guarded action rejects, the catch logs telemetry and resolves undefined; Promise.race therefore resolves undefined, and callers such as swarmPhase (battle.js:900) only compare against 'stall'. A thrown error in a move, shot, or ability is silently treated as success. As features add async paths, this hides real failures from both the player and the test net.

Restructure first, in this order: (a) replace the concatenated globals with ES modules and a bundler so name and load-order problems become compile errors; (b) replace show/mapScreen/newShip reassignment with named hooks; (c) move battle state out of the global B and make startSideTurn/endActivation/nextRound fully awaited; (d) collapse makeUnit and derive into one stat function; (e) move FLEET_LISTS and COMMANDS into data.js and delete the duplicates; (f) unify the build and smoke static checks over the same file list; (g) make the test build load the same Babylon version as the shipped build.

Lens 1 — Designer
1. OBSERVED — The shield/IFF colour law contradicts itself.
fx3d.js:2 says cyan is always shields, amber is always hull; green/red only marks friend or foe, but fx3d.js:3 says shields wear their faction's colour, and ships3d.js:5 gives corsair shield:new C3(.85,.16,.1) — a red shield that collides with enemy-red IFF. This breaks at-a-glance combat readability.

2. INFERRED — Voidsworn and Corsair are too close in palette to read apart at a glance.
data.js:111 has Voidsworn trim:'#c8501f',glow:'#ff7a2a'; data.js:115 has Corsair trim:'#b8402a',glow:'#e0402c'. Both are warm orange-red fleets, especially in dark scenes and small phone screenshots.

3. OBSERVED — Faction palettes have contradictory sources of truth.
data.js:111 says Voidsworn trim:'#c8501f', but ships3d.js:3 says trim:'#9c3a1c'; data.js:115 says Corsair trim:'#b8402a', but ships3d.js:5 says trim:'#a3542c'. The art direction cannot be judged consistently because the text disagrees with itself.

4. OBSERVED — “Rare” UI rarity reuses the exact shield cyan.
data.js:386 gives Rare col:'#62ccff'; fx3d.js:4 defines SHIELD_HEX='#62ccff'. A rarity label can therefore look like shield status, undermining the stated one-language colour system.

5. INFERRED — Phone dark-scene screenshots will suffer from dark translucent HUD over dark space.
head.html:41 uses background:var(--glass);backdrop-filter:blur(8px), while head.html:13 sets --bone3:#7d7768 and head.html:23 uses background:#04060c. Low-contrast secondary text on blurred dark panels is a screenshot legibility risk.

6. INFERRED — All six factions sit under one Gothic/imperial UI skin.
head.html:9 loads UnifrakturMaguntia, and head.html:17 sets --gothic:"UnifrakturMaguntia".... That suits Voidsworn, but the same UI frames bio-swarm, sail, and tomb factions, so menus do not reinforce faction read-apart.

7. INFERRED — Patron/boon colours reuse combat semantics.
data.js:388 gives Kharon col:'#e0b04a', close to hull amber; data.js:389 gives Vyre col:'#5fd4ff', close to shield cyan. In boon UI this competes with the hull/shield colour language defined at fx3d.js:4.

8. INFERRED — Phone screenshots lose fleet-level labelling by design.
fx3d.js:45 says the DOM label shows only for the ship in hand; with tiny hull arcs and faction-coloured shields, a phone screenshot can show many ships with little readable identity or damage state.

Not checked
I did not inspect actual rendered screenshots, shader compile output, device performance, audio, or source files absent from this pack such as sfx.js and wfx3d.js. I also did not audit every CSS breakpoint or every procedural ship-builder branch.

Lens 2 — Sci-Fi
1. OBSERVED — 40k vocabulary is not an influence; it is the base language.
data.js:40 — deathless:{gauss:'Gauss flayer',doom:'Doom arc',gsentry:'Gauss pylon',flak:'Gauss shredder'}};
Deathless is Necrons with the labels changed: Gauss flayer, Doom arc, Tomb-ark, reanimation, phylactery, Overlord, Cryptlord, Dynast. The same borrowing runs through Voidsworn (data.js:35: “Penance lance”, “Hellfire torpedo”, “Censer flak”), Chorus (data.js:38: bio-plasma, spore cloud, boarding tendrils), and Ithar (data.js:193: “Wraithbone compass”; data.js:24: “Shuriken cannon”). Warhammer 40,000.

2. OBSERVED — Hegemony Navy is Star Wars Imperial Navy pastiche.
data.js:113 — An imperial war-machine of wedge-hulled destroyers. Ion cannons strip shields; carriers launch fighter screens.
Wedge hulls, ion cannons, fighter screens, “Turbolaser” (data.js:11), “Proton magazine” (data.js:258), “Hyperdrive motivator” and “Durasteel weave” (data.js:264) are Star Wars vocabulary, not generic space-opera terms.

3. OBSERVED — Patron text calls every run a “crusade,” contradicting the faction run words.
data.js:384 — Each run four of six patrons watch the crusade
data.js:270 defines Hegemony runs as campaign, Corsair as raid, Chorus as hunt, Ithar as voyage, Deathless as reclamation. A Hegemony or Corsair player is still told the patrons watch their “crusade.”

4. INFERRED — Patrons have no explained cosmology or link to the six factions.
data.js:388 — quote:'Stand. Fire. Stand again.'
Kharon and the other five get a name, colour, theme, glyph and one-line quote. The pack never says what a patron is, why they watch runs, why their sigils appear on battles, or whether any faction worships or serves them. New player never gets this explained.

5. OBSERVED — “Choir” names a patron, a Chorus admiral, and the Chorus faction.
data.js:393 — short:'The Choir'
The patron is “The Ember Choir”; the Chorus admiral is “The Mending Choir” (data.js:224); the faction is “The Chorus” (data.js:118). The writing gives no rule for distinguishing patron, admiral, and faction.

6. INFERRED — “The Hunger Below” duplicates the Chorus faction’s identity without explaining the difference.
data.js:390 — theme:'Squadrons, boarding and feeding on the fallen'
The Chorus is A hungering bio-swarm (data.js:119) with boarding tendrils and spore mines. Is the Hunger the Chorus’s god, a separate entity, or the same hunger? The pack never says.

7. INFERRED — The war has no premise or stakes beyond one-line faction blurbs.
data.js:111 — Gothic crusader battleships. Heavy armor, lance batteries and broadsides that hit like a cathedral falling.
Every faction gets a single blurb. Nothing explains who the Voidsworn are, why they crusade, what the player commands, why the six factions fight, or what winning a run means in-world.

8. OBSERVED — Faction admirals are renamed copies of the same four commands.
data.js:214 — cmd:{id:'barrage',n:'Synchronized broadside'
Hegemony Kassar’s “Synchronized broadside” is the same barrage effect as Voidsworn Vex’s “Fire for effect” (data.js:209); the same duplication runs through hunt, scramble, iron, surge, and restore. The admirals have names and doctrines, but their Commands are not faction-specific writing.

9. OBSERVED — “HNS” is never expanded or explained.
data.js:320 — HNS Steadfast
Hegemony ship names use “HNS” throughout, but the pack never states what it stands for, whether Hegemony is human, or what its naval hierarchy means. A new player gets an acronym with no in-world meaning.

10. OBSERVED — “Iron” is spread across unrelated factions and patrons until it stops identifying anyone.
data.js:76 — The Iron Mandate
The Hegemony boss is “The Iron Mandate”; the Voidsworn patron is “Kharon, the Iron Saint” (data.js:388); the Corsair admiral is “Grax Ironjaw” (data.js:218); Thale has “Iron doctrine” (data.js:205); Deathless epithets include “the Iron-Hearted” (data.js:318). The word is used as a universal grimdark adjective, not a faction marker.

Not checked
I did not examine actual codex/tutorial text, dialogue, voice, or any lore files not included in this pack. I did not verify outside-franchise details beyond the names and terms quoted above.
